Polymer cards and original evidence

Polymers are retained because they are identified as polymers. Performance, purchase status and missing card fields do not decide retention.

Open each “Original evidence” section to see the source text and location. A matched quotation does not by itself verify the scientific interpretation.

Synthesis text

Reported in the supplied text

  • PTMG-HDI was synthesized via condensation of PTMG with HDI. Briefly, 2.5 g (1.25 mmol) of PTMG and 250 μL (1.5 mmol) of HDI were dissolved in 5 mL of CH₂Cl₂. 5 μL of DBTDL was added to the solution as a reaction catalyst. The final molar ratio of the monomers (PTMG:HDI) was 1:1.2. The mixture was stirred at 40 °C for 3 h in a N₂-filled glove box. Then, 5 mL of DMAc was added to the reaction mixture every 2 h 3 times repeated 3 times, and stirred for 12 h. Finally, a transparent PTMG-HDI solution was obtained.

    Tested/prepared specimen: transparent PTMG-HDI solution

    Original evidence (1 source passage)

    1. main · Methods / Preparation of PTMG-HDI, paragraph 25 · chunk src-0e0464a320ed4ac7c4a6

      PTMG-HDI was synthesized via condensation of PTMG with HDI. Briefly, 2.5 g (1.25 mmol) of PTMG and 250 μL (1.5 mmol) of HDI were dissolved in 5 mL of CH₂Cl₂. 5 μL of DBTDL was added to the solution as a reaction catalyst. The final molar ratio of the monomers (PTMG:HDI) was 1:1.2. The mixture was stirred at 40 °C for 3 h in a N₂-filled glove box. Then, 5 mL of DMAc was added to the reaction mixture every 2 h 3 times repeated 3 times, and stirred for 12 h. Finally, a transparent PTMG-HDI solution was obtained.

      Quote matched to converted source text

      Source XML converted with preserved superscripts/subscripts (jats_script_notation_v1).

      Source SHA-256: ffc5376b77b6b81e29c52134d1f96d0185752dd35d35cb48172b661b13c8e1a8

  • The PTMG-HDI and PTMG-HDI-BHDS polymer solutions were mixed with LiFSI in dimethylacetamide (DMAc) at a mass ratio of 2:1 (polymer: LiFSI) and stirred for 3 h. These viscous solutions were degassed under vacuum, cast into a Teflon mold, and then dried in a vacuum oven at 65 °C for 48 h to obtain the PEO/LiFSI, PTMG-HDI/LiFSI, and PTMG-HDI-BHDS/LiFSI electrolytes.

    Tested/prepared specimen: PTMG-HDI/LiFSI

    Original evidence (1 source passage)

    1. main · Methods / Preparation of the PEO/LiFSI, PTMG-HDI/LiFSI, and PTMG-HDI-BHDS/LiFSI electrolytes, paragraph 27 · chunk src-6524f9582cedc0d6c138

      The PTMG-HDI and PTMG-HDI-BHDS polymer solutions were mixed with LiFSI in dimethylacetamide (DMAc) at a mass ratio of 2:1 (polymer: LiFSI) and stirred for 3 h. These viscous solutions were degassed under vacuum, cast into a Teflon mold, and then dried in a vacuum oven at 65 °C for 48 h to obtain the PEO/LiFSI, PTMG-HDI/LiFSI, and PTMG-HDI-BHDS/LiFSI electrolytes.

      Quote matched to converted source text

      Source XML converted with preserved superscripts/subscripts (jats_script_notation_v1).

      Source SHA-256: ffc5376b77b6b81e29c52134d1f96d0185752dd35d35cb48172b661b13c8e1a8

Synthesis text

Reported in the supplied text

  • The synthesis of PTMG-HDI-BHDS involved the introduction of the chain extender BHDS into the PTMG-HDI. Briefly, 2.5 g (1.25 mmol) of PTMG, 500 μL (3.0 mmol) of HDI, and 5 μL of DBTDL were dissolved in 5 mL of CH₂Cl₂ and stirred at 40 °C for 1 h in a N₂-filled glove box, and then 200 μL (1.25 mmol) of BHDS was added to the above solution. The final molar ratio of the monomers (PTMG:HDI:BHDS) was 1:2.4:1. Subsequently, 5 mL of DMAc was added into the reaction mixture every 2 h and repeated 3 times, and stirred for 12 h to obtain the PTMG-HDI-BHDS solution.

    Tested/prepared specimen: PTMG-HDI-BHDS solution

    Original evidence (1 source passage)

    1. main · Methods / Preparation of PTMG-HDI-BHDS, paragraph 26 · chunk src-d1659b2c731d650cec05

      The synthesis of PTMG-HDI-BHDS involved the introduction of the chain extender BHDS into the PTMG-HDI. Briefly, 2.5 g (1.25 mmol) of PTMG, 500 μL (3.0 mmol) of HDI, and 5 μL of DBTDL were dissolved in 5 mL of CH₂Cl₂ and stirred at 40 °C for 1 h in a N₂-filled glove box, and then 200 μL (1.25 mmol) of BHDS was added to the above solution. The final molar ratio of the monomers (PTMG:HDI:BHDS) was 1:2.4:1. Subsequently, 5 mL of DMAc was added into the reaction mixture every 2 h and repeated 3 times, and stirred for 12 h to obtain the PTMG-HDI-BHDS solution.

      Quote matched to converted source text

      Source XML converted with preserved superscripts/subscripts (jats_script_notation_v1).

      Source SHA-256: ffc5376b77b6b81e29c52134d1f96d0185752dd35d35cb48172b661b13c8e1a8

  • The PTMG-HDI and PTMG-HDI-BHDS polymer solutions were mixed with LiFSI in dimethylacetamide (DMAc) at a mass ratio of 2:1 (polymer: LiFSI) and stirred for 3 h. These viscous solutions were degassed under vacuum, cast into a Teflon mold, and then dried in a vacuum oven at 65 °C for 48 h to obtain the PEO/LiFSI, PTMG-HDI/LiFSI, and PTMG-HDI-BHDS/LiFSI electrolytes.

    Tested/prepared specimen: PTMG-HDI-BHDS/LiFSI

    Original evidence (1 source passage)

    1. main · Methods / Preparation of the PEO/LiFSI, PTMG-HDI/LiFSI, and PTMG-HDI-BHDS/LiFSI electrolytes, paragraph 27 · chunk src-6524f9582cedc0d6c138

      The PTMG-HDI and PTMG-HDI-BHDS polymer solutions were mixed with LiFSI in dimethylacetamide (DMAc) at a mass ratio of 2:1 (polymer: LiFSI) and stirred for 3 h. These viscous solutions were degassed under vacuum, cast into a Teflon mold, and then dried in a vacuum oven at 65 °C for 48 h to obtain the PEO/LiFSI, PTMG-HDI/LiFSI, and PTMG-HDI-BHDS/LiFSI electrolytes.

      Quote matched to converted source text

Synthesis text

Reported in the supplied text

  • SPAN was synthesized according to a modified method previously reported⁶¹. A total of 1.0 g of polyacrylonitrile (PAN) and 3.5 g of S powder were uniformly mixed in 10 mL of ethanol by high-energy ball milling for 4 h. Then, SPAN with a sulfur content of 45.0 wt% was obtained after heating the composite at 300 °C for 450 min in a N₂ atmosphere.

    Tested/prepared specimen: SPAN

    Original evidence (1 source passage)

    1. main · Methods / Preparation of SPAN and S@CB, paragraph 28 · chunk src-98ed7d032c957d5e8b48

      SPAN was synthesized according to a modified method previously reported⁶¹. A total of 1.0 g of polyacrylonitrile (PAN) and 3.5 g of S powder were uniformly mixed in 10 mL of ethanol by high-energy ball milling for 4 h. Then, SPAN with a sulfur content of 45.0 wt% was obtained after heating the composite at 300 °C for 450 min in a N₂ atmosphere.

Synthesis text

Reported in the supplied text

  • The P(MMA-BA-(F-MA)) copolymer was prepared by emulsion polymerization. In brief, 2.5 wt% sodium dodecyl sulfate solution was prepared (as an emulsifier) with deionized water under N₂ at 60 °C. The mixture (MMA:BMA:F-MA = 1:2:1 (in molar ratio)) was added to the above solution under vigorous stirring for 1 h to form an emulsified solution. After adding ammonium persulfate (as an initiator) and stirring continuously for 6 h, the resulting emulsion was poured into 3 wt% Al₂(SO₄)₃ solution to yield a precipitate that was isolated by filtration and washed with hot deionized water (55 °C) in order to remove any impurities such as residual monomers and emulsifier. The P(MMA-BA-(F-MA)) copolymer was finally obtained by drying the purified precipitate in a vacuum oven at 60 °C for 12 h and kept in a desiccator for membrane preparation.

    Original evidence (1 source passage)

    1. main · 2. Materials and Methods / 2.3. Preparation of the Self-Healing Polymer Precursor, paragraph 9 · chunk src-189865c0a792b53247cf

      The P(MMA-BA-(F-MA)) copolymer was prepared by emulsion polymerization. In brief, 2.5 wt% sodium dodecyl sulfate solution was prepared (as an emulsifier) with deionized water under N₂ at 60 °C. The mixture (MMA:BMA:F-MA = 1:2:1 (in molar ratio)) was added to the above solution under vigorous stirring for 1 h to form an emulsified solution. After adding ammonium persulfate (as an initiator) and stirring continuously for 6 h, the resulting emulsion was poured into 3 wt% Al₂(SO₄)₃ solution to yield a precipitate that was isolated by filtration and washed with hot deionized water (55 °C) in order to remove any impurities such as residual monomers and emulsifier. The P(MMA-BA-(F-MA)) copolymer was finally obtained by drying the purified precipitate in a vacuum oven at 60 °C for 12 h and kept in a desiccator for membrane preparation.

      Quote matched to converted source text

      Source XML converted with preserved superscripts/subscripts (jats_script_notation_v1).

self-healing polymer electrolyte membrane

Polymer record · 10.3390/polym13234155

Original evidence (1 source passage)

  1. main · 2. Materials and Methods / 2.4. Preparation of the Self-Healing Polymer Electrolyte Membrane, paragraph 10 · chunk src-ee9d79e685026aec711f

    An optimal P(MMA-BA-(F-MA)):BMI-LiBOB self-healing polymer electrolyte composition was selected (the optimal molar ration of ((MMA-BA-(F-MA)):BMI = 2:1.1). Initially, a homogeneous solution of copolymer and lithium salt was prepared by mixing the components at 50 °C for 1 h. Subsequently, crosslinking agent was added to the mixture, after complete dissolution, the resulting viscous solution was cast with a doctor blade onto a glass plate, and then dried in vacuum at 90 °C for 24 h; finally, self-healing polymer electrolyte membrane was obtained.

    Quote matched to converted source text

    Source XML converted with preserved superscripts/subscripts (jats_script_notation_v1).

    Source SHA-256: cfd69ea12c067601a053396289614766ecd9d617fe86497483e61c116407fadc

Synthesis text

Reported in the supplied text

  • An optimal P(MMA-BA-(F-MA)):BMI-LiBOB self-healing polymer electrolyte composition was selected (the optimal molar ration of ((MMA-BA-(F-MA)):BMI = 2:1.1). Initially, a homogeneous solution of copolymer and lithium salt was prepared by mixing the components at 50 °C for 1 h. Subsequently, crosslinking agent was added to the mixture, after complete dissolution, the resulting viscous solution was cast with a doctor blade onto a glass plate, and then dried in vacuum at 90 °C for 24 h; finally, self-healing polymer electrolyte membrane was obtained.

    Tested/prepared specimen: SHPEM

Unresolved source discrepancy

thickness: The source reports multiple different thickness values for SHP electrolyte membrane forms (12 μm for electrochemical tests, about 15 μm for electrochemical performance tests, ~20 μm prepared membrane, ~10 μm transparent membrane, and about 25 μm healing demonstration) without fully resolving how these forms correspond.

Reported specimens: SHPEM, SHP electrolyte membranes used in electrochemical performance tests

        "entity_id": "p4",
        "value": "A typical polymerization procedure for P3 is described below. HO–PDMS–OH (23.0 g, 5.0 mmol) in a dried glass vessel equipped with a mechanical stirrer was heated in an oil bath at 100 °C under vacuum (<133 Pa) for 1 h to remove any moisture, and then cooled to 70 °C. IPDI (2.44 g, 11 mmol) and DBTDL (0.08 g) were dissolved in DMAc (20 mL), dropwise added into the vessel, and stirred for 3 h under a N2 atmosphere. After the synthesis of the pre-polymer, 4,4′-dithiodianiline (0.6 g, 2.5 mmol) and 4, 4′-bis(hydroxymethyl)-2,2′-bipyridine (0.465 g, 2.5 mmol) in 6 mL of dry DMAc were added to the pre-polymer solution. After stirring at 70 °C for another 3 h, the solution was added by methanol (3 mL), and further stirred for 30 min to ensure the consumption enough of isocyanate groups. Then, the solution was poured into a Poly tetra fluoroethylene (PTFE) plate that was subsequently put in a vacuum oven at 90 °C for 12 h to allow the reaction to complete. By dissolving the crude polymer in 40 mL of CH2Cl2 and then precipitating it into petroleum ether, we successfully obtained the purified P3 sample (16 g, yield 60%) as a yellow brown elastomer. The purification process was repeated three times.",
        "specimen_id": "p4-sample",
        "evidence": [
          {
            "chunk_id": "src-abdf1ced28f9b1e9fb83",
            "quote": "A typical polymerization procedure for P3 is described below. HO–PDMS–OH (23.0 g, 5.0 mmol) in a dried glass vessel equipped with a mechanical stirrer was heated in an oil bath at 100 °C under vacuum (<133 Pa) for 1 h to remove any moisture, and then cooled to 70 °C. IPDI (2.44 g, 11 mmol) and DBTDL (0.08 g) were dissolved in DMAc (20 mL), dropwise added into the vessel, and stirred for 3 h under a N2 atmosphere. After the synthesis of the pre-polymer, 4,4′-dithiodianiline (0.6 g, 2.5 mmol) and 4, 4′-bis(hydroxymethyl)-2,2′-bipyridine (0.465 g, 2.5 mmol) in 6 mL of dry DMAc were added to the pre-polymer solution. After stirring at 70 °C for another 3 h, the solution was added by methanol (3 mL), and further stirred for 30 min to ensure the consumption enough of isocyanate groups. Then, the solution was poured into a Poly tetra fluoroethylene (PTFE) plate that was subsequently put in a vacuum oven at 90 °C for 12 h to allow the reaction to complete. By dissolving the crude polymer in 40 mL of CH2Cl2 and then precipitating it into petroleum ether, we successfully obtained the purified P3 sample (16 g, yield 60%) as a yellow brown elastomer. The purification process was repeated three times."
          }
        ]
